LP step function:
```
def lp_direction(a, M, max_iter=None):
  n=len(a); b = M*np.ones(2*n-1)
  A = build_conv_matrix(a,n) # A @ g = a * g
  res = linprog(c=-np.ones(n), A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=(0,None), method='highs')
  if res.success: g=res.x; return g
```

```
def score_coord(a, i, t, c, S0, x):
  n=len(a); c2=c.copy() # copy n*2
  old_c = c
  # contributions j vector 0..n-1
  # positions = i + np.arange(n)
  # contributions vec: 2*a * (t-x) for j != i; for j=i: t*t - x*x
  add = 2*a*(t-x); add[i]=t*t - x*x
  c2[i:i+n]? positions from i to i+n-1; but some overlap. For j, affected c[i+j]. c2 at indices i..i+n-1 = c + add
  c2[i:i+n] = c[i:i+n] + add
  # Need ensure if 2i in range and j=i, add correct; yes
  # Unaffected max: max(c2) works because we overwritten affected, unaffected unchanged.
  S = S0 - x + t
  if S<=0: return inf
  return 2*n*c2.max()/(S*S)
```

Let's design code structure:
Top-level functions:
- `conv_square(p, n, nfft=None)`: compute c (length 2n-1).
- `full_score(c, n)`: 2*n*max(c)/(sum? if p normalized sum1, sum=1). For normalized p, score=2*n*max(c). For unnormalized, include sum. We can keep p normalized for optimization; return score = 2*n*max(c). Since sum p=1. Need ensure p sum not exactly 1 due to numerics; we can normalize.
- `compute_weights(c, n, mode, beta/delta)`: produce w length L (2n-1). Modes: 'softmax' with beta, or 'epsilon' active around max, or percentile.
- `grad_from_weights(p, w, n, nfft_g=None)`: compute grad = 2*conv(p[::-1],w)[n-1:n-1+n]. Use FFT length >=3n-2; p and w real. Use `np.fft.rfft`.
- `candidate_from_mirror(p, grad)`: q=normalize(exp(-eta*grad/grad_max)).
- `evaluate_score_fast(p)`: compute c and score.

- Compute c, maxu, score_fast = (2*n/L)*maxu? L=2n-1. Since score =2n*maxc = 2n*maxu/L. Equivalent. Use maxc. `maxc = max(c)`.

Algorithm for a run:
- p initial; J_cur = logsumexp(beta*u)
- current_best_in_run = p, J-run maybe.
- For iterations:
  - compute c,u,maxu,score; update global best if score lower.
  - compute w (softmax beta u).
  - J = beta*maxu + log(sum exp(beta(u-maxu))). (if u all maybe)
  - compute grad, gmax; if gmax=0 break.
  - q = normalize(exp(-eta*grad/gmax))
  - p_new = (1-m)*p + m*q
  - recompute c? We can compute J_new maybe with one FFT. If J_new < J - tol or (iteration % check==0 and score lower), accept; else reduce eta/m or random. But computing J_new requires c anyway. So each accepted or rejected step two c FFTs (old + new) if not accepted. Old c already computed. For rejection, we need new c to compute J_new. Could simply use a small fixed step and accept, then recompute next iteration old c. That is one c per iteration. If score bad, can detect later. Use batch rollback.

Batch approach:
```
p_save = p.copy()
for substep in range(batch):
  compute c,w,grad (one FFT pair? c and grad)
  update p
# after batch compute score; if score < score_save, keep and update; else p=p_save and maybe reduce step.
```
Inside substep, after update p, next substep c uses new. So one c+grad per substep. No candidate eval. If batch fails, rollback. This is efficient. Need choose batch small (e.g., 20) to adapt.

But if we don't evaluate score each substep, we might wander. We can check score every, say, 20 substeps. Good.

Let's design an adaptive simulator:
- We have time budget. Use a global best (p,score). For a schedule of (n, restarts), allocate time slices e.g., 30-60s each. Each slice run `optimize_length(n, time_limit, start_p=None, seed)`. Returns best p for that n. We collect global best, choose min exact score.
- For each n, initial starts:
  * uniform p (as baseline; may not improve due stationary; but with broad active may escape if weights not beta too high? uniform gradient nonzero if active broad; could improve. Good.)
  * exponential increasing: p_i ∝ exp(i/γ), γ ~ n/2 or n/4. Normalize.
  * exponential decreasing.
  * random exponential with drift.
  * global best if same n? Not.
  * height_sequence_1 if length n normalized.
- Maybe long start from coarser optimized sequence upscaled: If we found good p_m for m≈√n? Could upsample? Not necessary.

`optimize_length(n, limit, yaw...)`:
- Initialize local best as uniform or best start, score=2.
- For each start until limit:
  - p = start normalized; ensure positive (eps) maybe. Score fast, update.
  - Run mirror descent with batch/rollback:
    `eta = 0.5`, `m=0.5`, `beta = 3` increasing to 20 over time.
    Max maybe 5000 substeps if time. Use while time_check.
    At each substep:
      c, maxc = conv_square(p)
      if time: break
      # update global? maybe every 10
      if maxc >0:
        u = L*maxc? Actually u = L*c; maxu
        beta_current = base_beta maybe.
        z = beta_current*(u - maxu); w=exp(z); w/=sum
        grad=conv_grad(p,w)
        gmax=grad.max(); if gmax<=0: add small random
        g=grad/gmax
        q=exp(-eta*g)
        if q sum small: fallback uniform
        normalize q
        p_new = (1-m)*p + m*q
        # optional add tiny noise to break symmetry: p_new = (1-noise)*p_new + noise*random? Could disrupt.
        p = p_new; normalize
    - Check score every e.g. 50 substeps: if score < best_run: save; else if many no improvement, rollback to save and reduce eta/beta? Maybe.
- Use coordinate descent at end for n<=600? Let's implement simple coordinate local search with a few passes if time remains.

```
def mirror_update(p, beta, delta):
  c,u,maxu = compute
  score = ...

  w = top_active(u, maxu, delta) # maybe if delta=0 includes max only; if sum=0 w uniform
```

```
import numpy as np, time, math

def next_power_of_two(x):
    return 1 << (max(1,int(x)-1).bit_length())

def normalize_positive(x, eps=1e-12):
    x = np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.clip(x,0,None)
    s = x.sum()
    if s <= 0: return np.full(x.shape,1.0/x.size)
    x = x/s
    # add eps? Keep positive? If zeros, okay. For log updates, zeros cause issues. We can maintain floor: x = (1-eps)*x + eps/x.size? But zeros may useful. For mirror q, if p_i=0, exp(-eta grad) positive -> allows entering. p as mixture with q may have positive if q>0. If start with zeros, q positive. Good.
    return x
```

`conv_square(p,n, nfft)`:
```
if nfft is None: nfft=next_power_of_two(2*n-1)
F=np.fft.rfft(p,nfft)
c=np.fft.irfft(F*F,nfft)[:2*n-1]
c=np.real(c)
# sym? ensure nonnegative? for nonnegative p, small negative possible
c=np.maximum(c,0.0) # But if small negative due to FFT, clipping may affect max? not.
return c
```
For n large, `F*F` can amplify; but p sum1, F[0]=1, other O(√n), square O(n); inverse c O(1/n). Fine.

`conv_grad(p,w,n,ngfft)`:
```
if ngfft is None: ngfft=next_power_of_two(3*n-2)
pr = p[::-1]
Fr = np.fft.rfft(pr, ngfft)
Fw = np.fft.rfft(w, ngfft)
c = np.fft.irfft(Fr*Fw, ngfft)
start=n-1
grad = 2.0 * c[start:start+n]
return np.maximum(grad,0.0)? grad should nonnegative (w,p nonneg). Clip small negative.
```
Check length: pr length n, w length L=2n-1. conv length n+L-1=3n-2. start n-1 to 2n-2? For i=0..n-1, index n-1+i: max 2n-2, within length 3n-2 (last index 3n-3). Good.

Score fast:
```
def fast_score_normalized(p):
  n=len(p); L=2*n-1
  c=conv_square(p,n)
  maxc=float(c.max())
  # if p sum not 1, include sum
  s=p.sum()
  if s<=1e-12: return np.inf
  return float(2*n*maxc/(s*s))
```
But if p normalized sum1, fine. We can always compute s.

Maybe p from mixing has sum 1 within. Use exact formula for non normalized.

`make_weights(u, mode, beta=5.0, delta=0.2)`:
```
L=len(u); maxu=float(u.max())
if maxu<=0: return np.ones(L)/L
if mode=='softmax':
  z=beta*(u-maxu)
  # if z min too low, no underflow because >= -beta*range; range maybe <10, beta<100 -> exp min tiny okay
  w=np.exp(z)
  sw=w.sum()
  if sw==0 or not finite: w = (u >= maxu - 0.0).astype(float); sw=w.sum(); w/=sw
  else: w/=sw
elif mode=='epsilon':
  w=(u >= maxu - delta*maxu).astype(float)
  sw=w.sum()
  if sw==0: w=np.zeros(L); w[argmax]=1
  else w/=sw
return w
```
For `delta=0.0`, active just max; if multiple equal due uniform? for uniform only center. Could be stationary? We'll use delta>0.

```
def one_mirror_transition(p, prev_score, beta, deltas, etas):
  c,u,maxc,score = compute_current
  best_p=p; best_score=score; improved=False
  for delta in deltas:
    w=make_epsilon(u, maxu, delta)
    grad=conv_grad(...); gmax=grad.max(); gmin=grad.min(); range = max(gmax-gmin)
    if range <= 1e-14:
       continue
    g=(grad-gmin)/range
    for eta in etas:
      q=exp(-eta*g); normalize
      cand = 0.7*q + 0.3*p? maybe try mix 0.5 first
      sc=fast_score(cand)
      if sc < best_score - 1e-10:
         return cand, sc, True
  # try softmax beta if no epsilon
  return ...
```

Maybe a deterministic "descent" with surrogate no candidate eval: For each transition, after weight/grad/q, just set p = p_new and run for batch, then check. This halves FFT. Let's design robust function:
```
def mirror_batch(p, n, time_limit, target_score, beta, delta, eta, mix):
  start_p=p; start_score=fast_score(p)
  no_improve=0
  while time...:
    c,u,maxc,score=...
    if score < start_score: start_p=p.copy(); start_score=score; no_improve=0; update global
    else no_improve += 1
    if no_improve > threshold: break/rollback
    w=...
    grad=...
    g=...
    q=...
    p = mix*q + (1-mix)*p
    normalize
  return p, best_score
```
This only computes score each substep (c FFT) and grad (another FFT). No extra candidate c. If batch fails, we can rollback to `start_p` and adjust hyperparams. But substeps may converge to bad; periodic rollback if no improvement.

Since c computed each substep can be used to update best. We can accept p as long as score <= previous + tolerance? But may drift. Use batch: after K substeps, compare best score observed; if no observed improvement, rollback K? Actually track min score in run; accept p at that min. If after some period no lower score than saved, rollback to saved. Simpler:
```
best_p_run=p; best_score_run=score
save_p=p; save_score=score; steps_since_save=0
for substep:
  update p
  score = compute (c+grad anyway)
  if score < best_score_run - 1e-9: best_score_run=score; best_p_run=p.copy(); save_p=p.copy(); save_score=score; steps_since_save=0; update global
  else: steps_since_save+=1
  if steps_since_save > max_step_no_improve: p=save_p; reduce eta or change delta; reset steps_since_save=0
```
But because c computed before update for weights, after update we need next loop score. This means every substep c+grad. If we rollback, c not wasted? okay.

We can choose adaptive hyperparameters:
- `delta`: active width. If current maxu is far above target, use larger delta to move mass; near target, smaller. `delta = 0.02 + 0.5*max(0, maxu-target_u)`. target_u = target_score * (2n-1)/(2n) ≈ target. Or fixed 0.2.
- `eta`: strength. Start 1.0; if no improvement for many, increase or explore.
- `mix`: Start 0.5; if no improvement, lower to 0.2 (gentler) or higher to 1.0 (bolder). We can cycle.

Since objective nonconvex, random restarts crucial. We can run `optimize_length` with a time limit and several restarts. For each restart, choose `mode`: epsilon widths and etas.

Let's further improve mirror by using normalized active weights and step size based on reducing top u to target. Could perhaps converge to score 1.4? Let's design carefully to ensure it doesn't stall. We can include multiple random modes: epsilon widths, softmax beta, random active subset (top coefficient plus random neighbors), and occasional block perturbation.

Potential problem: Multiplicative update p_i ∝ p_i e^{-η g_i} can push p to sparse spikes (if g differences large), causing score increase. We can control with mix and eta. Use `g = (grad - min)/range` in [0,1], q=exp(-η g). This makes min g=1, max g=e^{-η}; relative change factor e^{-η}. With η~1, factor 0.37. If mix=0.5, final change factor between 1 and0.8. Moderate.

We can select eta adaptively: want reduce max sum roughly by 10-20%. If grad range small, eta effectively small.

Algorithm `transition(p, hyper)`:
```
c,u,maxc,score = current
if score <= best: update
# compute a suggested target delta
if maxu > target_u: delta = 0.05 + 0.5*(maxu-target_u)
else: delta = 0.02
# if delta too large cap 0.5
w = (u >= maxu - delta).astype(float)
w /= w.sum()
grad = conv_grad
gmax=grad.max(); gmin=grad.min(); rangee = gmax-gmin
if rangee <= 1e-10:
    # add random perturbation
    p = normalize(p * (1 + 0.05*rng.normal))
    return p, score
g = (grad-gmin)/rangee
for eta in [0.4,0.8,1.4,2.2]:
   q=normalize(exp(-eta*g))
   for mix in [0.3,0.6,0.9]:
     cand=normalize((1-mix)*p + mix*q)
     sc = fast_score(cand)
     if sc < score - 1e-9: return cand, sc, True
# optionally try softmax and smaller delta
return p,score,False
```
This evaluates up to 12 candidates per transition. Might be okay if transitions limited. We can reduce: choose best eta/mix via a two-stage line search: first try eta=0.8,mix=0.5; if no improve, try eta=1.5,mix=0.7; if no, eta=0.4,mix=0.3; if no, softmax. Let's use 4 candidates.

Since `fast_score(cand)` requires `conv_square`. We can cache? no.

Could use approximate score from c? For cand we need c. We'll compute.

Number of candidates per transition high. But transitions can stop when time. For n=512, candidate eval c length 1024, cheap. For 10000 transitions maybe okay.

We can also compute gradient and candidate from same weight; c for candidate different. OK.

To improve convergence, after a successful transition, we can keep current weight/delta. If no improvement, change delta smaller/larger.

Maybe implement `optimize_length` as a loop of transitions with random hyperparameters:
```
best_p, best_s = start
p = start
for mode in modes:
  p = start
  for iter...
```
Need avoid too many code but okay.

Alternative use a population: maintain a few best p for length n, mutate best and random elites. But global best enough.

Let's design `run_local_search(n, time_limit, rng, start_p=None, target_score)`:
- If n too large for coordinate, use mirror.
- Determine nfft/ng.
- Initialize a small list of starts: uniform, linear ramp (1+0.5*2*x?) anti-sym min, random Dirichlet, reversed linear, maybe global best if same length.
- For each start with enough time:
   - p=normalize(start); score fast; update best.
   - Run transitions for a subtime (e.g., 20s per start or until no improve). Use randomized hyperparameters:
     * choose delta from [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2] or adaptive.
     * choose eta from [0.4,0.8,1.2,1.8]
     * choose mix from [0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9]
   - Use exact evaluate_sequence occasionally for candidate if score < best_global + small and time allows.
- Return best for length.

But code simplicity? We can do one main loop with a schedule.

Let's specify exact functions.

```
def fast_score(p):
    a = np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=1e-12: return np.inf
    # scale to sum1 for speed? We can divide by s to keep magnitudes.
    q=a/s
    nfft=next_power_of_two(2*n-1)
    F=np.fft.rfft(q,nfft)
    c=np.fft.irfft(F*F,nfft)[:2*n-1]
    maxc=float(c.max())
    return float(2.0*n*maxc) # since q sum1
```
If we always compute score with q sum1, we should normalize p each step. Good. `fast_score_normalized` assumes sum1; we can normalize before. To avoid extra sum? we have.

```
def score_for_t(a,i,t,c,S):
  n=len(a)
  add = 2.0 * a * (t - old)  # length n
  diag = t*t - old*old
  # positions i+j for j=0..n-1
  # Need if j==i: add[i] should be diag (not 2a_i*(t-old)). So set add[i]=diag.
  add[i]=diag
  # c affected from i to i+n-1 length n
  # Copy c and update only affected?
  affected = c[i:i+n] + add
  max_aff = float(affected.max())
  # max_unaff: need max of c outside positions i..i+n-1. If copy c and set affected to -inf then max, O(n). Use prefix/suffix? For n small, copy.
  tmp = c.copy()
  tmp[i:i+n] = affected
  return 2*n*tmp.max()/(S - old + t)**2
```

Let's detail `local_search` for efficiency:
```
def local_search(p, score, n, time_limit, rng, benchmark_nfft=None):
    deadline = time.monotonic()+time_limit
    nfft = next_power_of_two(2*n-1); L1=2*n-1
    ngfft = next_power_of_two(3*n-2)
    best_p = p.copy(); best_s = float(score)
    save_p = p.copy(); save_s = best_s
    no_improve=0
    step_no=0
    # maybe initial current score computed by caller? We can compute c once inside.
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       # compute current state
       c,u,maxc = compute_c_u(p,n,nfft)
       current_s = 2*n*maxc  # p sum1
       if current_s < best_s - 1e-10:
          best_s=current_s; best_p=p.copy(); save_p=p.copy(); save_s=current_s; no_improve=0; print
       elif time for exact...
       else:
          no_improve += 1
       if no_improve > 40:
          # try random shake from save_p
          # 3 random shakes
          found=False
          for _ in range(3):
             shake = rng.normal(size=n)*0.1 + 0.1*(np.linspace(0,1,n)-0.5)
             cand=normalize(save_p + save_p*(shake)) # relative
             sc=fast_score(cand,n,nfft)
             if sc < best_s:
                p=cand; best_s=sc; best_p=cand.copy(); save_p=cand.copy(); no_improve=0; found=True; break
          if not found:
             # change p to save_p and maybe break start
             p=save_p.copy()
             if time monotonic start? maybe return
             # If many shake fail, stop this start
             break
       # choose active weights: adaptive delta based on current_s
       maxu = maxc*L1
       # target_u = target * L1/(2n) ≈ target
       if current_s > 1.55: delta = rng.choice([0.1,0.2,0.3])
       elif current_s > 1.5: delta = rng.choice([0.05,0.1])
       else: delta = rng.choice([0.02,0.05])
       # maybe sometimes softmax
       use_softmax = rng.random()<0.2
       beta = rng.choice([2,4,6,10])
       if use_softmax: w=softmax_weights(u,maxu,beta)
       else: w=epsilon_weights(u,maxu,delta)
       # a couple variations of delta? Instead compute one grad per loop. Candidate evals can use q with different eta/mix.
       grad=conv_grad(p,w,n,ngfft)
       gmax=grad.max(); gmin=grad.min(); rng_ = gmax-gmin
       if not finite or rng_ <= 1e-12:
          # add symmetric? random shake
          p=normalize(p * (1 + 0.05*rng.normal(size=n)))
          continue
       g=(grad-gmin)/rng_
       # Candidate evaluation: choose 3 etas/mix combinations
       improved=False
       # prefer combinations that reduce score
       combos = [(0.5,0.5), (1.0,0.7), (2.0,0.4), (0.8,0.9)]
       rng.shuffle(combos) # but top-level no lambda? okay.
       for eta,mix in combos:
           q=exp_candidate(g, eta)
           cand = normalize((1-mix)*p + mix*q)
           sc=fast_score(cand,n,nfft)
           if sc < current_s - 1e-10:
               p=cand; current_s=sc; save_p=cand.copy(); save_s=current_s; no_improve=0; improved=True; break
       if not improved:
           # try a direct small gradient step with mix=0.1? Maybe already combos. no_improve++
           no_improve += 2
    return best_p, best_s
```
But note: after computing c/u at top, if we accept candidate, `current_s` updated. The next loop recomputes c for new p. Good.

For 3809, maybe 6*16k (2 FFTs? fast_score one, top c one, grad one) ~3 FFT 8k? Actually grad 16k, c 8k. 6 FFT 8k/16k per loop maybe 10ms; 200 loops 2s? okay.

`exp_candidate(g, eta)`:
```
e = -eta * g
min_e = e.max()? e <=0. If eta > 50, underflow. Clip e to [-50,0]
q = np.exp(e)
qsum = q.sum()
if qsum <=0 or not finite: q=np.ones(n)
else q/=qsum
return q
```
Since g in [0,1], eta up to 2, no underflow. Good.

```
def param_search(n, time_limit, rng):
  x = np.linspace(0,1,n)
  basis = stack [x, x^2, x^3, x^2(1-x), sin(pi x/2), sin(pi x), cos(pi x), exp(3x)-exp(3)] maybe normalized.
  theta_best = zeros
  best_p = ones
  best_s = 2
  scale = 1.0
  for iterations until time:
     for attempt in range(5):
        theta = theta_best + scale*rng.normal(d)
        p = positive_param(theta,basis)
        s = fast_score(p)
        if s<best_s: best..., scale*=1.1 (cap)
        elif s<best_s+0.01: scale*=0.95
        else scale*=0.9
  return best_p
```

```
def build_conv_matrix(a,n):
  L=2*n-1
  A=np.empty((L,n), dtype=np.float64)
  for k in range(L):
    s=max(0,k-n+1); e=min(n,k+1)
    A[k, s:e] = a[s:e]
  return A
```

Helper functions:
1. `_next_pow2(x)`.
2. `_normalize_nonnegative(x)`:
```
a=np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
a=np.clip(a,0.0,None)
s=a.sum()
if not finite or s<=1e-15:
   return np.full(a.size, 1.0/a.size)
return a/s
```
3. `_conv_square(p, n, nfft=None)`:
```
if nfft is None: nfft=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
F=np.fft.rfft(p, nfft)
c=np.fft.irfft(F*F, nfft)[:2*n-1]
c = np.real(c)
# Remove tiny negatives; small negative at ends may not affect max but for u weights avoid.
c[np.abs(c)<1e-15]=0.0
return c
```
Could values be negative less than -1e-12 for large n due FFT? Maybe clip with `np.maximum(c,0)`.
4. `_grad_from_weights(p,w,n,ngfft=None)`:
```
L=len(w); if L!=2*n-1: w=w[:L]
pr=p[::-1]
Fr=np.fft.rfft(pr,ngfft); Fw=np.fft.rfft(w,ngfft)
conv=np.fft.irfft(Fr*Fw, ngfft)
start=n-1
g=2.0*conv[start:start+n]
g=np.maximum(np.real(g),0.0)
return g
```
5. `_weights_mixed(u, maxu, beta, delta)`:
```
L=len(u); z=beta*(u-maxu); e=np.exp(z); sw=e.sum(); if sw<=...: ws=none
else ws=e/sw
thresh=maxu - delta*maxu
we=(u >= thresh).astype(float); sme=we.sum(); if sme<=0: we=np.zeros(L); we[argmax]=1 else we/=sme
w = 0.5*ws + 0.5*we
w/=w.sum()
return w
```
Maybe if delta=0.0, we active only max. We'll mix.
6. `_exp_candidate(g, eta)`:

```
e=-eta*g

return q
```
7. `_fast_score(p, n, nfft=None)`:

Maybe `_normalize` in every score expensive. We can maintain normalized. For candidate we normalize; for current p normalized. So `fast_score_keep_sum(p)` computes s=sum(p), if s not 1 use s. No clipping. Use `p.sum()`.
```
def _fast_score(p,n,nfft=None):
    s=float(p.sum())
    if s<=1e-12: return inf
    if abs(s-1)>1e-6:
       q=p/s
    else: q=p
    c=_conv_square(q,n,nfft)
    return 2*n*float(c.max())
```
But if we scale p/s, c scaled by 1/s^2, score formula for q sum1 is 2n maxc_q. Since maxc_q = maxc_p/s^2, same. Good. For speed, assume p normalized; but small drift. We can not scale each time and just use sum to adjust: `maxc = max(_conv_square(p,n,nfft))`; score=2n*maxc/(s*s)`. That is one FFT without scaling. For p normalized, same. Do that.
```
def _fast_score(p,n,nfft=None):
   s=p.sum()
   if s<=...: return inf
   c=_conv_square(p,n,nfft)
   return float(2*n*c.max()/(s*s))
```
This uses p as is. Good. If p has very small sum? no.

10. `_lp_direction(p, M)`:
```
n=p.size; A=_build_convolution_matrix(p,n)
A_scaled=A/M
maxg=10.0
A_full=np.vstack((A_scaled, np.ones((1,n))))

c_obj=-np.ones(n)
res=linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A_full, b_ub=b, bounds=(0,None), method='highs')
if not res.success: return None
x=np.clip(res.x,0,None)
if x.sum()<=0: return None
return x/x.sum(), x.sum()
```

But if no mix improves but gsum big, maybe small mix? We tried. Break.

12. `_make_starts(n, rng, height_arr=None, limit_starts=8)`:
```
starts=[]
# uniform
starts.append(np.ones(n))
# linear ramps
x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
lambdas=[0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9]
for lam in lambdas:
  p=1+lam*(2*x-1)
  p=np.maximum(p,1e-8)
  starts.append(p)
  starts.append(p[::-1])
# quadratic/sine features
starts.append(1 + 0.5*(x**2 - x/3)) # smooth
starts.append(1 + 0.5*np.sin(np.pi*x) - ...?)
# random antisym
for trial in range(2):
   r=rng.normal(size=n)
   # antisym
   if n%2==0:
      half=n//2; r2=np.empty(n); r2[:half]=rng.normal(size=half); r2[half:]=-r2[:half][::-1]
   else:
      half=n//2; r2=np.empty(n); r2[:half]=rng.normal(size=half); r2[half]=0; r2[half+1:]=-r2[:half][::-1]
   r2=r2/(np.max(np.abs(r2))+1e-12)
   p=1+0.7*r2
   p=np.maximum(p,1e-8)
   starts.append(p)
# random Dirichlet
starts.append(rng.dirichlet(np.full(n,1.0)))
starts.append(rng.dirichlet(np.full(n,0.5)))
# shifted block
shift=0.25+n? p = 1 + 0.4*( (x>0.5)?1:0? ) with mean zero: p = 1 + 0.4*( (x>0.5) - 0.5*? ). Need mean zero: p=1 + a*(mask) + b*(1-mask) choose mean 0: mean=a*frac + b*(1-frac). If frac=0.5 and a=0.5,b=-0.5. p=1+0.5*(mask-0.5). For mask>0.5? Use offset c=0.3: mask = x>0.5+c? Mean not 0.5. Could simply p=1 + 0.3*(x-0.5) +0.3*(>0.5)*(?)...
```

```
def _exact_score(arr):
    a = np.asarray(arr,dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size==0: return np.inf
    a = np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    # ensure sum>=0.01 by scaling if too small
    s = a.sum()
    if s < 0.01:
        a = a + 0.01/a.size
    try:
        seq = [float(x) for x in a]
        val = evaluate_sequence(seq)
    except Exception:
        return np.inf
    if not np.isfinite(val): return np.inf
    return float(val)
```

```
    no_improve=0
    iter=0
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
       c=_conv_square(p,n,nfft); maxc=float(c.max()); sx=float(p.sum())
       current_s=2*n*maxc/(sx*sx)
       if current_s < best_s -1e-11:
           best_s=current_s; best_p=p.copy(); no_improve=0; print
       else:
           no_improve += 1
       if no_improve >= 80:
           # try a few shakes from best
           escaped=False
           for _ in range(5):
              bias = 0.15*rng.normal(size=n) + 0.15*(np.linspace(0,1,n)-0.5)
              cand = best_p * (1 + 0.1*bias/(np.std(bias)+1e-12))
              cand = _normalize_nonnegative(cand)
              sc = _fast_score(cand,n,nfft)
              if sc < best_s:
                 p=cand; best_p=cand.copy(); best_s=sc; no_improve=0; escaped=True; break
           if not escaped:
              break
       u = (c/(sx*sx))*(2*n-1)
       maxu=float(u.max())
       # weights
       delta=...
       beta=...
       w=...
       g=...
       if range bad: 
          # random default perturbation
          bias=0.05*rng.normal(size=n)
          p=_normalize_nonnegative(best_p*(1+0.03*bias))
          no_improve += 10
          continue
       g=(g-gmin)/grange
       # candidate combos
       improved=False
       for eta,mix in [(0.5,0.5),(1.0,0.6),(1.8,0.4),(0.8,0.8)]:
           q=_exp_candidate(g, eta)
           cand=(1-mix)*p+mix*q
           cand=_normalize_nonnegative(cand)
           sc=_fast_score(cand,n,nfft)
           if sc < current_s - 1e-11:
              p=cand; best_p=cand.copy(); best_s=sc; no_improve=0; improved=True; break
       if not improved: no_improve += 1
       iter += 1
    return best_p,best_s
```

```
def _lp_search(start_p, time_limit, rng, tag=""):
   if not HAVE_SCIPY: return start_p, _fast_score(start_p)
   n=start_p.size
   p=_normalize_nonnegative(start_p)
   # min positive
   eps=1e-12
   p=(1-eps)*p + eps/n
   score=_fast_score(p)
   best_p=p.copy(); best_s=score
   deadline=time.monotonic()+time_limit
   nfft=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
   for it in range(5):
      if time.monotonic()>deadline: break
      p=_normalize_nonnegative(p)
      c=_conv_square(p,n,nfft); M=float(c.max())
      if M<=0: break
      g,gsum=_lp_direction(p,M)
      if g is None or gsum <= 1.0 + 1e-6: break
      improved=False
      for mix in (0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9):
         cand=(1-mix)*p + mix*g
         cand=_normalize_nonnegative(cand)
         sc=_fast_score(cand,n,nfft)
         if sc < best_s - 1e-9:
            best_s=sc; best_p=cand; improved=True; break
      if not improved:
         break
      p=best_p
   return best_p,best_s
```

```
def _multiplicative_p(q, g, eta):
   if g.size==0... 
   gmin=g.min(); grange=g.max()-gmin
```

`_weights_mixed` implementation with tuple sort? no sort.
```
def _weights_mixed(u, maxu, beta, delta):
    L = len(u)
    if L == 0 or not np.isfinite(maxu) or maxu <= 0:
        return np.ones(L)/L if L else None
    z = beta*(u - maxu)
    e = np.exp(z)
    se = e.sum()
    if se > 0 and np.isfinite(se): ws = e/se
    else:
        ws = np.zeros(L); ws[int(np.argmax(u))]=1.0
    thresh = maxu - delta*maxu
    we = (u >= thresh).astype(float)
    swe = we.sum()
    if swe <= 0:
        we = np.zeros(L); we[int(np.argmax(u))]=1.0
    else:
        we /= swe
    w = 0.5*ws + 0.5*we
    sw = w.sum()
    if sw <=0: w=we
    else: w/=sw
    return w
```
If delta negative? no.

If mask true (low part): p=1 - a*c =1-0.5*0.428=0.786; else p=1 + a =1.5. Mean1. Good.

```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    start = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = max(60.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = start + total_budget - 5.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    height = None
    h = globals().get('height_sequence_1', None)
    if h is not None:
       try:
          arr=np.asarray(h,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
          arr=np.clip(arr,0,1000)
          if arr.size>0 and np.all(np.isfinite(arr)) and float(arr.sum())>0:
             height=arr
       except: pass
    # plan
    total_time = max(30.0, total_budget - 8.0)
    base_fracs = {16:0.006,32:0.014,64:0.025,128:0.035,256:0.11,512:0.26,1024:0.31,2048:0.09}
    if height is not None and 64 <= height.size <= 4096:
       base_fracs[int(height.size)] = 0.13
    order=[16,32,64,128,256,512,1024,2048]
    if height is not None:
       hn=int(height.size)
       if hn not in order:
          # insert maybe after 2048? Use order plus hn
          order.append(hn)
    plan=[]
    for n in order:
       if n in base_fracs:
          plan.append((n, base_fracs[n]))
    # if no 3809? includes.
    frac_sum=sum(f for _,f in plan)
    if frac_sum<=0:
       plan=[(256,1.0)]
    # scale sizes by budget maybe if height length >4096, ignore.
    history=[]
    best_fast=np.inf; best_fast_arr=[]; best_exact=np.inf; best_exact_arr=None
    idx_counter=0
    for n, frac in plan:
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
       alloc = frac/frac_sum * total_time
       # cap maybe if n large not too much? alloc as is.
       if alloc < 5: continue
       print(f"Starting n={n}, alloc={alloc:.1f}s, current best fast={best_fast if finite else inf}")
       p,s = _optimize_length(n, alloc, rng, height)
       if p is not None:
          arr=np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64)
          arr=np.clip(arr,0,1000)
          fs = s if np.isfinite(s) else _fast_score(arr,n)
          history.append((fs, idx_counter, arr.copy()))
          idx_counter +=1
          if fs < best_fast:
             best_fast=fs; best_fast_arr=arr
          if time.monotonic() < deadline - 2:
             ex=_exact_score(arr)
             if ex < best_exact:
                best_exact=ex; best_exact_arr=arr.copy(); print("Exact improved:",ex,n)
    # final exact evaluate top history
    # sort by fs
    hist_sorted = sorted(history) # tuple (fs,idx,arr)
    checked=0
    for fs, idx, arr in hist_sorted:
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 1: break
       if fs > best_exact + 0.05 and best_exact < 1.55: # skip if far? We can evaluate top 10
          if checked > 15: break
       ex=_exact_score(arr)
       checked +=1
       if ex < best_exact:
          best_exact=ex; best_exact_arr=arr.copy(); print(...)
    # fallbacks
    fallbacks=[]
    if height is not None:
       fallbacks.append(height)
    fallbacks.append(np.ones(256))
    fallbacks.append(np.array([1.0,1.0,0.0,1.0]))
    fallbacks.append(np.array([1.0,1.0,0.0,0.0,1.0,1.0]))

       if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 0.5: break
       # normalize? _exact_score clips but okay.
       ex=_exact_score(arr)
       if ex < best_exact:
          best_exact=ex; best_exact_arr=arr.copy()
    if best_exact_arr is None or best_exact >= np.inf:
       # use best_fast if any
       if best_fast_arr is not None:
          best_exact_arr=best_fast_arr
       else:
          best_exact_arr=np.ones(256)
    # normalize final
    final=np.asarray(best_exact_arr,dtype=np.float64)
    final=np.clip(final,0,1000)
    final=final[~np.isnan(final) & ~np.isinf(final)] if needed? We can `final=np.nan_to_num(final, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)`
    if final.size==0 or final.sum()<=0.01:

    final=final/final.sum()
    return [float(x) for x in final]
```

```
import numpy as np
import time
try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
    HAVE_LINPROG = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_LINPROG = False

TARGET_SCORE = 1.5027
```

Functions:
```
def _next_pow2(x):
    x = int(x)
    if x <= 1: return 1
    return 1 << (x - 1).bit_length()
```
Check x=2 ->1 <<1=2. x=3 ->1<<2=4. Good.

`_normalize_nonnegative`:
```
def _normalize_nonnegative(x):
    a=np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size==0:
       return np.ones(1,dtype=np.float64)
    a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<=1e-15:
       return np.full(a.size,1.0/a.size,dtype=np.float64)
    return a/s
```

`_conv_square`:
```
def _conv_square(p, n, nfft=None):
    if nfft is None: nfft=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
    F=np.fft.rfft(p, nfft)
    c=np.fft.irfft(F*F, nfft)[:2*n-1]
    c=np.real(c)
    c=np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
    c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
    return c
```
`np.where` with scalar 0 returns array. Good.

`_grad_from_weights`:
```
def _grad_from_weights(p,w,n,ngfft=None):
    if ngfft is None: ngfft=_next_pow2(3*n-2)
    pr=p[::-1]
    Fr=np.fft.rfft(pr,ngfft); Fw=np.fft.rfft(w,ngfft)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(Fr*Fw,ngfft)
    start=n-1
    g=2.0*np.real(conv[start:start+n])
    g=np.where(np.isfinite(g),g,0.0)
    g=np.maximum(g,0.0)
    return g
```

`_weights_mixed` as above.

`_multiplicative_update`:
```
def _multiplicative_update(p,g,eta):
    if p.size==0: return p.copy()
    g=np.asarray(g,dtype=np.float64)
    gmax=float(g.max()); gmin=float(g.min()); rng_ = gmax-gmin
    if not np.isfinite(rng_) or rng_ <= 1e-14:
        return p.copy()
    gh=(g-gmin)/rng_
    e=-eta*gh
    e=np.clip(e,-50.0,0.0)
    w=p*np.exp(e)
    s=float(w.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<=1e-300:
       return p.copy()
    w=w/s
    return w
```

`_fast_score`:
```
def _fast_score(p,n,nfft=None):
    a=np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size==0: return np.inf
    s=float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<=1e-15: return np.inf
    c=_conv_square(a,n,nfft)
    mx=float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(mx): return np.inf
    return float(2.0*n*mx/(s*s))
```
For p normalized, score. If p has very large values, sum maybe overflow? We normalize starts; candidates clip 1000. If not normalized and large, sum^2 overflow? Could be. Use scale? Since we pass normalized, fine. To be safe, if max(a)>1e150, normalize first? We can do:
```
if abs(s-1)>1e-3 and (s>1e100 or s<1e-100): a=a/s
```
But not needed. Could add general scaling to avoid overflow:
```
if s > 1e100 or s < 1e-100: a=a/s
```
Then score=2n*maxc (since normalized). Simpler: always compute with normalized? `a=a/s` each call copies but fine. Let's do inside fast_score:
```
q=a/s if abs(s-1)>1e-6 else a
c=... maxc; return 2*n*maxc
```
But if s not 1 and we don't scale, maxc formula uses s. Scaling might under/overflow? Either. Use formula.
If s huge, s*s overflow. Our p normalized; keep formula but maybe no. We can robust:
```
if s > 1e100 or s < 1e-100:
    q=a/s
    c=...; return 2*n*c.max()
else:
    c=...; return 2*n*c.max()/(s*s)
```
Good.

`_make_top_starts`: Need count. No nested.
```
def _make_top_starts(n,count=5,rng=None,height=None):
    if rng is None: rng=np.random.default_rng(0)
    starts=[]
    ... append
    scored=[]
    nfft=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
    for idx,a in enumerate(starts):
       arr=_normalize_nonnegative(a)
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)): continue
       s=_fast_score(arr,n,nfft)
       if np.isfinite(s): scored.append((s, idx, arr))
    if not scored:
       return [np.ones(n,dtype=np.float64)/n]
    scored.sort()
    res=[]
    for _,_,a in scored:
       res.append(a)
       if len(res)>=count: break
    return res
```
In appends, ensure arrays not too spiky? Some p all zero? `_normalize` handles.

`_local_search`: Need use global `TARGET_SCORE`? We can.
```
def _local_search(start_p,n,time_limit,rng,tag=""):
    if time_limit <=0: return _normalize_nonnegative(start_p), _fast_score(...)
    deadline=time.monotonic()+time_limit
    p=_normalize_nonnegative(start_p)
    nfft=...; ngfft=...
    best_s=_fast_score(p,n,nfft); best_p=p.copy()
    current_s=best_s; no_improve=0; it=0
    etas=(0.3,0.6,1.0,1.6,2.2)
    mixes=(0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9,1.0)
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       c=_conv_square(p,n,nfft)
       sSum=float(p.sum())
       mx=float(c.max())
       if not np.isfinite(mx) or sSum<=0: break
       current_s = 2.0*n*mx/(sSum*sSum)
       if current_s < best_s - 1e-11:
          best_s=current_s; best_p=p.copy(); no_improve=0
          if it % 100 == 0: print(...)
       else:
          no_improve += 1
       if no_improve >= 70:
          escaped=False
          for _ in range(6):
             bias = 0.25*rng.normal(size=n) + 0.25*(np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)-0.5)
             std=float(bias.std())
             if std<=0: std=1.0
             cand = best_p * (1.0 + 0.15*bias/std)
             cand = _normalize_nonnegative(cand)
             sc=_fast_score(cand,n,nfft)
             if sc < best_s:
                p=cand; best_p=cand.copy(); best_s=sc; no_improve=0; escaped=True; break
          if not escaped:
             break
       c_iso = c/(sSum*sSum)
       L=2*n-1
       u = c_iso * L
       maxu=float(u.max())
       if not np.isfinite(maxu) or maxu<=0: break
       if current_s > 1.75:
          delta=float(rng.choice((0.15,0.25,0.35)))
       elif current_s > 1.55:
          delta=float(rng.choice((0.08,0.12,0.2)))
       elif current_s > TARGET_SCORE:
          delta=float(rng.choice((0.04,0.07,0.12)))
       else:
          delta=float(rng.choice((0.015,0.03,0.06)))
       beta=float(rng.choice((2.0,4.0,6.0,8.0,12.0)))
       w=_weights_mixed(u,maxu,beta,delta)
       g=_grad_from_weights(p,w,n,ngfft)
       gmax=float(g.max()); gmin=float(g.min()); grange=gmax-gmin
       if not np.isfinite(grange) or grange <= 1e-13:
          bias=0.1*rng.normal(size=n)
          p=_normalize_nonnegative(best_p*(1.0+0.05*bias))
          no_improve += 5
          continue
       improved=False
       for _ in range(3):
          eta=float(rng.choice(etas)); mix=float(rng.choice(mixes))
          up=_multiplicative_update(p,g,eta)
          cand=(1.0-mix)*p + mix*up
          cand=_normalize_nonnegative(cand)
          sc=_fast_score(cand,n,nfft)
          if sc < current_s - 1e-11:
             p=cand; best_p=cand.copy(); best_s=sc; no_improve=0; improved=True
             break
       if not improved:
          no_improve += 1
       it += 1
    return best_p,best_s
```
Potential issue: If current_s < best_s, update best, but `current_s` computed from p. We then use weights from p. Good.
If no_improve increments at top even when current_s == best_s? Yes. If best updated at top, no_improve reset. If a candidate improved previous iteration, p changed; top next iter will see current_s == best_s? It is same as best, but condition `current_s < best_s -1e-11` false, so no_improve increments immediately. That means even after an improvement, next iteration counts as no_improve unless it improves further. That's okay; it measures consecutive iterations without a better score than the previous best. But if improvements are every few iterations, reset when best updated at top? Wait after candidate improves, we update best_s to sc and set p=cand. At next top, current_s=best_s; not less, so no_improve becomes 1. It won't reset until new improvement. Thus no_improve counts iterations since last strict improvement. Good.

But best updated at top only if current_s < previous best. Since p is always candidate improved, top current_s equals best_s exactly? Due recomputing may differ by 1e-15. If slightly less, reset; else no. No matter.

In shake, condition `sc < best_s` only downhill. If not, break. Good.

One concern: `_fast_score(cand)` uses FFT; if cand has sum not 1 but normalized by `_normalize_nonnegative`, sum1. Good.

`_optimize_length`:
```
def _optimize_length(n,time_limit,rng,height):
    if n<2 or time_limit<=0: return None, np.inf
    t0=time.monotonic()
    count = 6 if n<=128 else 4 if n<=512 else 3
    starts=_make_top_starts(n,count,rng,height)
    best_p=None; best_s=np.inf
    # optionally sort starts by initial score? _make returns sorted.
    remaining = time_limit
    for idx,p in enumerate(starts):
        if time.monotonic() - t0 > time_limit: break
        if remaining <= 3: break
        # slice: give remaining divided among remaining starts, but at least 5s if possible
        slice_time = max(3.0, remaining / max(1, len(starts)-idx))
        if slice_time > remaining: slice_time = remaining
        p2,s2=_local_search(p,n,slice_time,rng,f"start{idx} n={n}")
        if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_s:
           best_s=s2; best_p=p2
        remaining = time_limit - (time.monotonic()-t0)
    # LP
    if HAVE_LINPROG and best_p is not None and n <= 512:
       rem = time_limit - (time.monotonic()-t0)
       if rem > 20:
          p3,s3=_lp_search(best_p, min(rem, 120.0), rng)
          if np.isfinite(s3) and s3 < best_s:
             best_s=s3; best_p=p3
    return best_p,best_s
```
If first start consumes all remaining due slice_time = remaining/(1) etc. Good.

Maybe local_search can run slice_time but if it breaks early due no_improve, we get remaining. Good.

`_lp_search` as above. Need pass nfft maybe to avoid compute? Use internal.

```
def _lp_search(start_p, time_limit, rng):
    if not HAVE_LINPROG: return start_p, _fast_score(start_p)
    n=start_p.size
    p=(1-1e-9)*_normalize_nonnegative(start_p)+1e-9/n
    best_s=_fast_score(p,n)
    best_p=p.copy()
    deadline=time.monotonic()+time_limit
    nfft=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
    for _ in range(4):
       if time.monotonic()>=deadline: break
       p=_normalize_nonnegative(p)
       p=(1-1e-9)*p+1e-9/n
       c=_conv_square(p,n,nfft); M=float(c.max())
       if M<=0 or not finite: break
       g,_ = _lp_direction(p,M)
       if g is None: break
       gs=float(g.sum())
       if gs <= 1.0 + 1e-5: break
       improved=False
       for mix in (0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9):
          cand=(1-mix)*p + mix*g
          cand=_normalize_nonnegative(cand)
          sc=_fast_score(cand,n,nfft)
          if sc < best_s - 1e-9:
             best_s=sc; best_p=cand; improved=True; break
       if not improved: break
       p=best_p
       # add small perturbation to break complement?
       p=_normalize_nonnegative(best_p*(1+1e-4*rng.normal(size=n)))
    return best_p,best_s
```

`_lp_direction`:
```
def _lp_direction(p,M):
    n=p.size
    if M<=0: return None,0
    L=2*n-1
    A=np.empty((L,n), dtype=np.float64)
    A.fill(0.0)
    for j in range(n):
        A[j:j+n, j] = p[j]
    A_scaled = A / M
    A_full = np.vstack((A_scaled, np.ones((1,n), dtype=np.float64)))
    b = np.full(L+1, 1.0, dtype=np.float64)
    b[-1] = 10.0
    cobj = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n
    try:
       res = linprog(cobj, A_ub=A_full, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
    except Exception:
       try:
          res = linprog(cobj, A_ub=A_full, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds)
       except Exception:
          return None,0.0
    if res is None or not getattr(res,'success',False):
       return None,0.0
    x=np.asarray(res.x,dtype=np.float64)
    if x.size != n or not np.all(np.isfinite(x)): return None,0
    x=np.clip(x,0.0,None)
    s=float(x.sum())
    if s <=0: return None,0
    return x/s, s
```
`bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n` creates list. Good. `A` construction with p maybe p with min 1e-9. For n=512, columns loop 512. Good.

Potential memory in `_lp_direction` for n=512 fine. If time_limit small, still build. OK.

```
       n = max(64, final.size)
       final=np.ones(n,dtype=np.float64)
    final = final / float(final.sum())
    return [float(x) for x in final]
```

```python
"""
Search non-negative sequences with an FFT-based multiplicative local search.

The objective is
    score(a) = 2*n*max(conv(a,a)) / (sum(a))^2
for non-negative a.  This implementation keeps probabilities normalized
(sum=1), works in the symmetric convolution coefficients, and uses
multiplicative-direction updates driven by weights placed on large
convolution coefficients.  It uses many asymmetric random restarts,
adaptive active-coefficient widths, occasional random shakes, and optional
scipy-LP refinement for moderate sizes.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
    HAVE_LINPROG = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_LINPROG = False

TARGET_SCORE = 1.5027


def _next_pow2(x):
    x = int(x)
    if x <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (x - 1).bit_length()


def _normalize_nonnegative(x):
    a = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-15:
        return np.full(a.size, 1.0 / a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    return a / s


def _conv_square(p, n, nfft=None):
    if nfft is None:
        nfft = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    f = np.fft.rfft(p, nfft)
    c = np.fft.irfft(f * f, nfft)[:2 * n - 1]
    c = np.real(c)
    c = np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def _grad_from_weights(p, w, n, ngfft=None):
    if ngfft is None:
        ngfft = _next_pow2(3 * n - 2)
    pr = p[::-1]
    fp = np.fft.rfft(pr, ngfft)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w, ngfft)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(fp * fw, ngfft)
    start = n - 1
    g = 2.0 * np.real(conv[start:start + n])
    g = np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
    g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
    return g


def _weights_mixed(u, maxu, beta, delta):
    L = len(u)
    if L == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.isfinite(maxu) or maxu <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64) / L

    z = beta * (u - maxu)
    e = np.exp(z)
    se = float(e.sum())
    if np.isfinite(se) and se > 0.0:
        ws = e / se
    else:
        ws = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        ws[int(np.argmax(u))] = 1.0

    thresh = maxu - delta * maxu
    we = (u >= thresh).astype(np.float64)
    swe = float(we.sum())
    if swe <= 0.0:
        we = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        we[int(np.argmax(u))] = 1.0
    else:
        we /= swe

    w = 0.5 * ws + 0.5 * we
    sw = float(w.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(sw) or sw <= 0.0:
        w = we
    else:
        w /= sw
    return w


def _multiplicative_update(p, g, eta):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    g = np.asarray(g, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.size == 0:
        return p.copy()
    gmax = float(g.max())
    gmin = float(g.min())
    rng = gmax - gmin
    if not np.isfinite(rng) or rng <= 1e-14:
        return p.copy()
    gh = (g - gmin) / rng
    expo = -float(eta) * gh
    expo = np.clip(expo, -50.0, 0.0)
    q = p * np.exp(expo)
    s = float(q.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return p.copy()
    return q / s


def _fast_score(p, n, nfft=None):
    a = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size != n or a.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-15:
        return np.inf

    if s > 1e100 or s < 1e-100:
        q = a / s
    else:
        q = a

    c = _conv_square(q, n, nfft)
    mx = float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(mx):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * mx)


def _exact_score(arr):
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0 or a.size > 60000:
        return np.inf

    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        a = np.full(a.size, 1.0 / a.size, dtype=np.float64)
        s = 1.0
    if s < 0.01:
        a = a * (0.01 / s)

    try:
        func = globals().get("evaluate_sequence", None)
        if func is None:
            return np.inf
        seq = [float(x) for x in a]
        val = func(seq)
        val = float(val)
        if not np.isfinite(val):
            return np.inf
        return val
    except Exception:
        return np.inf


def _make_top_starts(n, count=5, rng=None, height=None):
    if rng is None:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(0)

    starts = []
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)

    # Basic smooth asymmetric starts.
    starts.append(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))
    for lam in (0.20, 0.35, 0.50, 0.70, 0.90):
        p = 1.0 + lam * (2.0 * x - 1.0)
        starts.append(np.maximum(p, 1e-8))
        starts.append(np.maximum(p[::-1], 1e-8))

    p = 1.0 + 0.5 * (x - 0.5) + 0.2 * (x * x - 1.0 / 3.0)
    starts.append(np.maximum(p, 1e-8))
    p = 1.0 + 0.4 * x + 0.2 * (1.0 - x) * x
    starts.append(np.maximum(p, 1e-8))

    # Off-center step starts.
    for frac, amp in ((0.25, 0.6), (0.40, 0.5), (0.60, 0.5)):
        mask = (x < frac).astype(np.float64)
        c = frac / max(1e-12, 1.0 - frac)
        p = 1.0 + amp * (mask - c * (1.0 - mask))
        starts.append(np.maximum(p, 1e-8))

    # Anti-symmetric random starts help break the symmetric >= 2 barrier.
    for _ in range(3):
        r = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        half = n // 2
        if half > 0:
            first = rng.normal(size=half)
            r[:half] = first
            r[n - half:] = -first[::-1]
        if n % 2 == 1:
            r[n // 2] = 0.0
        rn = float(np.max(np.abs(r)))
        if rn > 0.0:
            r /= rn
        p = 1.0 + 0.8 * r
        starts.append(np.maximum(p, 1e-8))

    # Probabilistic random starts.
    for alpha in (0.35, 1.0, 2.5):
        starts.append(rng.dirichlet(np.full(n, alpha, dtype=np.float64)))

    if n <= 64:
        for _ in range(3):
            b = (rng.random(n) < 0.5).astype(np.float64)
            if float(b.sum()) > 0.0:
                starts.append(b)

    # Provided sequence of the same length, if useful.
    if height is not None and height.size == n:
        h = _normalize_nonnegative(height)
        starts.append(h)
        starts.append(h * (1.0 + 0.05 * rng.normal(size=n)))

    scored = []
    nfft = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    for i, a in enumerate(starts):
        arr = _normalize_nonnegative(a)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
            continue
        s = _fast_score(arr, n, nfft)
        if np.isfinite(s):
            scored.append((s, i, arr))

    if not scored:
        return [np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)]

    scored.sort()
    out = []
    for _, _, a in scored:
        out.append(a)
        if len(out) >= count:
            break
    return out


def _local_search(start_p, n, time_limit, rng, tag=""):
    if time_limit <= 0.0:
        p = _normalize_nonnegative(start_p)
        return p, _fast_score(p, n)

    deadline = time.monotonic() + float(time_limit)
    p = _normalize_nonnegative(start_p)
    nfft = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    ngfft = _next_pow2(3 * n - 2)

    best_p = p.copy()
    best_s = _fast_score(p, n, nfft)
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0

    etas = (0.3, 0.55, 0.9, 1.4, 2.0)
    mixes = (0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 0.9, 1.0)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        c = _conv_square(p, n, nfft)
        sx = float(p.sum())
        mx = float(c.max())
        if not np.isfinite(mx) or sx <= 0.0:
            break

        current_s = float(2.0 * n * mx / (sx * sx))
        if current_s < best_s - 1e-11:
            best_s = current_s
            best_p = p.copy()
            no_improve = 0
            if it < 8 or it % 200 == 0:
                print(f"{tag} n={n} fast={current_s:.8f} it={it}")
        else:
            no_improve += 1

        # Random shake from the best point if stalled; accept only downhill.
        if no_improve >= 70:
            escaped = False
            for _ in range(6):
                bias = 0.25 * rng.normal(size=n) + 0.25 * (np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n) - 0.5)
                st = float(bias.std())
                if st <= 0.0:
                    st = 1.0
                cand = best_p * (1.0 + 0.18 * bias / st)
                cand = _normalize_nonnegative(cand)
                sc = _fast_score(cand, n, nfft)
                if sc < best_s:
                    p = cand
                    best_p = cand.copy()
                    best_s = sc
                    no_improve = 0
                    escaped = True
                    break
            if not escaped:
                break

        # Working coefficients normalized so average is 1.
        L = 2 * n - 1
        c_iso = c / (sx * sx)
        u = c_iso * L
        maxu = float(u.max())
        if not np.isfinite(maxu) or maxu <= 0.0:
            break

        if current_s > 1.75:
            delta = float(rng.choice((0.15, 0.25, 0.35)))
        elif current_s > 1.55:
            delta = float(rng.choice((0.08, 0.12, 0.20)))
        elif current_s > TARGET_SCORE:
            delta = float(rng.choice((0.04, 0.07, 0.12)))
        else:
            delta = float(rng.choice((0.015, 0.03, 0.06)))

        beta = float(rng.choice((2.0, 4.0, 6.0, 8.0, 12.0)))
        w = _weights_mixed(u, maxu, beta, delta)
        g = _grad_from_weights(p, w, n, ngfft)

        gmax = float(g.max())
        gmin = float(g.min())
        grange = gmax - gmin

        if not np.isfinite(grange) or grange <= 1e-13:
            bias = 0.10 * rng.normal(size=n)
            p = _normalize_nonnegative(best_p * (1.0 + 0.05 * bias))
            no_improve += 5
            continue

        improved = False
        for _ in range(3):
            eta = float(rng.choice(etas))
            mix = float(rng.choice(mixes))
            up = _multiplicative_update(p, g, eta)
            cand = (1.0 - mix) * p + mix * up
            cand = _normalize_nonnegative(cand)
            sc = _fast_score(cand, n, nfft)
            if sc < current_s - 1e-11:
                p = cand
                best_p = cand.copy()
                best_s = sc
                no_improve = 0
                improved = True
                break

        if not improved:
            no_improve += 1

        it += 1

    return best_p, best_s


def _optimize_length(n, time_limit, rng, height):
    if n < 4 or time_limit <= 0.0:
        return None, np.inf

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    best_p = None
    best_s = np.inf

    count = 6 if n <= 128 else (4 if n <= 512 else 3)
    max_gen = 10 if n <= 64 else (7 if n <= 256 else (4 if n <= 1024 else 2))

    generation = 0
    while generation < max_gen and (time.monotonic() - t0) < max(5.0, float(time_limit) - 5.0):
        starts = _make_top_starts(n, count, rng, height)
        if not starts:
            break

        for idx, p in enumerate(starts):
            if time.monotonic() - t0 >= float(time_limit):
                break
            rem = float(time_limit) - (time.monotonic() - t0)
            if rem <= 3.0:
                break
            slice_time = rem / max(1, len(starts) - idx)
            p2, s2 = _local_search(p, n, slice_time, rng, tag=f"n={n} gen={generation}")
            if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_s:
                best_s = s2
                best_p = p2

        generation += 1

    if HAVE_LINPROG and best_p is not None and n <= 512:
        rem = float(time_limit) - (time.monotonic() - t0)
        if rem > 25.0:
            p3, s3 = _lp_search(best_p, min(rem, 150.0), rng)
            if np.isfinite(s3) and s3 < best_s:
                best_s = s3
                best_p = p3

    return best_p, best_s


def _build_convolution_columns(p, n):
    L = 2 * n - 1
    A = np.empty((L, n), dtype=np.float64)
    A.fill(0.0)
    for j in range(n):
        A[j:j + n, j] = p[j]
    return A


def _lp_direction(p, M):
    n = int(p.size)
    if M <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(M):
        return None, 0.0

    L = 2 * n - 1
    A = _build_convolution_columns(p, n)
    A = A / M
    Afull = np.vstack((A, np.ones((1, n), dtype=np.float64)))
    b = np.full(L + 1, 1.0, dtype=np.float64)
    b[-1] = 10.0
    c = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n

    res = None
    try:
        res = linprog(c, A_ub=Afull, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method="highs")
    except Exception:
        try:
            res = linprog(c, A_ub=Afull, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds)
        except Exception:
            res = None

    if res is None or not getattr(res, "success", False):
        return None, 0.0

    x = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
    if x.size != n:
        return None, 0.0
    x = np.nan_to_num(x, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    x = np.clip(x, 0.0, None)
    s = float(x.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return None, 0.0
    return x / s, s


def _lp_search(start_p, time_limit, rng):
    if not HAVE_LINPROG:
        p = _normalize_nonnegative(start_p)
        return p, _fast_score(p)

    n = int(start_p.size)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + float(time_limit)
    p = _normalize_nonnegative(start_p)
    p = (1.0 - 1e-9) * p + (1e-9 / n)

    best_p = p.copy()
    best_s = _fast_score(p)
    nfft = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)

    for _ in range(4):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break

        p = _normalize_nonnegative(p)
        p = (1.0 - 1e-9) * p + (1e-9 / n)

        c = _conv_square(p, n, nfft)
        M = float(c.max())
        if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0.0:
            break

        g, gsum = _lp_direction(p, M)
        if g is None or gsum <= 1.0 + 1e-5:
            break

        improved = False
        for mix in (0.1, 0.2, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 0.9):
            cand = (1.0 - mix) * p + mix * g
            cand = _normalize_nonnegative(cand)
            s = _fast_score(cand, n, nfft)
            if s < best_s - 1e-9:
                best_s = s
                best_p = cand.copy()
                improved = True
                break

        if not improved:
            break

        p = best_p
        p = _normalize_nonnegative(best_p * (1.0 + 1e-4 * rng.normal(size=n)))

    return best_p, best_s


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = max(60.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = t0 + total_budget - 6.0

    try:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    except Exception:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(12345)

    height = None
    h = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if h is not None:
        try:
            arr = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
            arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if arr.size > 0 and np.all(np.isfinite(arr)) and float(arr.sum()) > 0.0:
                height = arr
        except Exception:
            height = None

    total_time = max(30.0, total_budget - 8.0)

    base_fracs = {
        16: 0.005,
        32: 0.012,
        64: 0.023,
        128: 0.035,
        256: 0.10,
        512: 0.27,
        1024: 0.32,
        2048: 0.095,
    }

    if height is not None:
        hn = int(height.size)
        if 16 <= hn <= 4096:
            base_fracs[hn] = max(base_fracs.get(hn, 0.0), 0.18)

    order = [16, 32, 64, 128, 256, 512, 1024, 2048]
    if height is not None:
        hn = int(height.size)
        if hn not in order:
            order.append(hn)

    plan = []
    for n in order:
        if n in base_fracs and 8 <= n <= 4096:
            plan.append((n, float(base_fracs[n])))

    if not plan:
        plan = [(256, 1.0)]

    frac_sum = sum(fr for _, fr in plan)
    if frac_sum <= 0.0:
        plan = [(256, 1.0)]
        frac_sum = 1.0

    history = []
    best_exact = np.inf
    best_exact_arr = None
    hist_idx = 0

    for n, fr in plan:
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break

        alloc = fr / frac_sum * total_time
        if alloc < 5.0:
            continue

        print(f"Subsearch n={n} alloc={alloc:.1f}s")
        p, fs = _optimize_length(n, alloc, rng, height)

        if p is not None:
            arr = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
            arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
            arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if arr.size > 0 and np.isfinite(fs):
                history.append((float(fs), hist_idx, arr.copy()))
                hist_idx += 1

            if arr.size > 0 and arr.size <= 5000 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
                ex = _exact_score(arr)
                if ex < best_exact:
                    best_exact = ex
                    best_exact_arr = arr.copy()
                    print(f"Exact improved: {ex:.8f} n={n}")

    if history:
        hist_sorted = sorted(history)
        checked = 0
        for fs, _, arr in hist_sorted:
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 1.0:
                break
            if checked >= 25:
                break
            if best_exact < 1.50 and fs > best_exact + 0.02 and checked > 10:
                break
            if arr.size <= 5000:
                ex = _exact_score(arr)
                checked += 1
                if ex < best_exact:
                    best_exact = ex
                    best_exact_arr = arr.copy()
                    print(f"Final exact improvement: {ex:.8f}")

    fallbacks = []
    if height is not None and 0 < height.size <= 5000:
        fallbacks.append(height)
    fallbacks.append(np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64))
    fallbacks.append(np.array([1.0, 1.0, 0.0, 1.0], dtype=np.float64))
    fallbacks.append(np.array([1.0, 1.0, 0.0, 0.0, 1.0, 1.0], dtype=np.float64))

    for arr in fallbacks:
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 0.2:
            break
        ex = _exact_score(arr)
        if ex < best_exact:
            best_exact = ex
            best_exact_arr = arr.copy()

    if best_exact_arr is None:
        if history:
            best_exact_arr = history[0][2]
        else:
            best_exact_arr = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64)

    final = np.asarray(best_exact_arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    final = np.nan_to_num(final, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    final = np.clip(final, 0.0, 1000.0)

    if final.size == 0:
        final = np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64)

    s = float(final.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.01:
        m = max(64, int(final.size))
        final = np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        final = final / s

    return [float(x) for x in final]
```
